18. Reproduction guide

Prerequisites

  • A rooted Android device or an emulator image with writable /system (the app's root detection is defeated by script 01, but Frida itself needs root or a repackaged gadget build).
  • frida-server matching your Frida version, listening on the default port. Rename the binary and use a non-default port if you want to exercise the detection bypass more realistically.
  • Host tooling: pip install frida-tools (this analysis used frida 17.18.0 / frida-tools 14.10.4).
  • Burp Suite or mitmproxy with a CA certificate installed as a system trust anchor (user CAs are not trusted on API 24+).

Step 1 — static recovery (no device needed)

# 1. Unpack
unzip "TopFollow_v845-Beta (1).apk" -d apk/

# 2. Signing certificate (no apksigner / JDK required)
python3 - <<'PY'
import struct, hashlib
d = open("TopFollow_v845-Beta (1).apk","rb").read()
m = d.rfind(b"APK Sig Block 42")
off = 0x9ce628                                  # v2 pair: u64 len at off-8, id 0x7109871a at off
plen = struct.unpack_from('<Q', d, off-8)[0]
body = d[off+4 : off-8+plen]
i = body.find(b'\x30\x82')                       # first DER SEQUENCE
clen = struct.unpack_from('>H', body, i+2)[0] + 4
der = body[i:i+clen]
print("cert SHA-256:", hashlib.sha256(der).hexdigest())
PY

# 3. Native secrets: single-byte XOR sweep over libtopfollow.so
python3 - <<'PY'
import re
d = open("apk/lib/arm64-v8a/libtopfollow.so","rb").read()
for run in re.finditer(rb'[\x20-\x7e](6,)', d):
    s = run.group()
    for k in (0x55, 0x5A):
        out = bytes(c ^ k for c in s)
        if re.search(rb'https?://|\.php|frida|xposed|/su$|su$|maps|sha256/', out):
            print(hex(k), run.start(), out.decode('latin1'))
PY

# 4. Base64-obfuscated endpoints
grep -aoE 'd\.o\("[A-Za-z0-9+/=](8,)"\)' out/all/*.java | sort -u \
  | sed 's/.*"\(.*\)".*/\1/' | while read b; do echo -n "$b" | base64 -d; echo; done

Step 2 — dynamic analysis

# Baseline: boot the app cleanly past every protection
frida -U -f com.nivaroid.topfollow \
      -l dynamic-lab/00_common.js \
      -l dynamic-lab/01_anti_tamper_killer.js \
      -l dynamic-lab/02_ssl_pinning_bypass.js

# Add the layer you are investigating:
#   03 - Instagram private-API traffic        04 - credential harvest
#   05 - coin economy                         06 - task verification
#   07 - native/JNI dump                      08 - backend + ServerCheck

# Full stack
frida -U -f com.nivaroid.topfollow \
      -l dynamic-lab/00_common.js \
      -l dynamic-lab/01_anti_tamper_killer.js \
      -l dynamic-lab/02_ssl_pinning_bypass.js \
      -l dynamic-lab/03_instagram_api_intercept.js \
      -l dynamic-lab/04_credential_theft.js \
      -l dynamic-lab/05_coin_economy_bypass.js \
      -l dynamic-lab/06_task_verification_bypass.js \
      -l dynamic-lab/07_native_jni_dumper.js \
      -l dynamic-lab/08_backend_traffic_and_servercheck.js

Step 3 — verify the headline exploits

  1. Credentials (CRED-01/02/03). Log in with a throwaway Instagram account, then call rpc.exports.dump() in script 04. Confirm the plaintext password, the Bearer IGT:2:… token and any s_k seed in the JSON output and in /data/data/com.nivaroid.topfollow/files/topfollow_dump.json.
  2. Coin fraud (BIZ-01/02). With script 06 attached, run a task that Instagram rejects. Confirm the log line get_coin false -> true and that order/syncOrder.php is still POSTed. Watch the coin counter in the notification rise.
  3. Free purchase (BIZ-03/05). With script 05 attached, open any Buy dialog. Confirm DeviceModel.getCoin() 12 -> 999999999 and that the Buy button is now clickable.
  4. MITM (TRAN-01/02). Point the device at Burp. Confirm requests to top.nivafollower.app and to b.i.instagram.com decrypt, and that the Token header carries the live Instagram session (CRED-04).
  5. Redirect (TRAN-02). Set OVERRIDE_BASE_URL at the top of script 08 to your own host. Confirm the app rebuilds its Retrofit against your server and sends instagramLogin.php there.
  6. Backup leak (STOR-02). Trigger a cloud backup or a device-to-device transfer, then inspect the restored t_f_d_b_f_v_c with sqlite3: select username, u_w, u_a from instagram_accounts; and select u_n, u_p, s_k from two_factors;. Decrypt the values with the Python routine in section 11.
  7. Repackaging (INTE-01). Modify and re-sign the APK with a different key, spawn it with script 01, and confirm the app reaches its main screen with SHA-256 digest <new> -> forced to d845591e… in the log.

19. Remediation roadmap

Read this first. Most of what follows cannot be fixed with client-side engineering, because the product's core function — automating Instagram accounts that users do not control, in exchange for a currency whose earning is verified on the client — is itself the vulnerability. Items P0-1 through P0-4 are genuine security fixes; items under “existential” are not fixable without changing what the app does. This section is written as if the goal were to make the app safe to operate, which requires both.

P0 — stop the credential bleeding (days)

  1. Stop accepting Instagram passwords. Remove u_w, remove two_factors.u_p and s_k, remove instagramLogin.php. Migrate to Instagram's official OAuth with PKCE and store only a scoped, expiring token. CRED-01, CRED-02, CRED-05
  2. Stop sending the session token to your own backend. Delete d.t()'s Token and Active-Id headers. The backend never needs them. CRED-04
  3. Remove the WebView cookie harvester. If a web login is genuinely required, use Custom Tabs so the browser owns the session and the app cannot read it. CRED-03, STOR-07
  4. Fix backup immediately. Either set android:allowBackup="false" or populate both rule files with explicit exclusions for the database and TOPFVC_Shared. This is a one-line change that closes an offline mass-exposure path. STOR-02
  5. Encrypt local storage. SQLCipher for Room under an AndroidKeyStore key; EncryptedSharedPreferences for the rest. STOR-01, STOR-03, BIZ-05, CRYP-01

P1 — make the money path server-authoritative (weeks)

  1. Delete get_coin. Compute the reward entirely server-side. BIZ-01
  2. Delete order_value from the request. Look it up by order_id. BIZ-02
  3. Verify completion independently. After a claim, fetch the target's follower/like/comment state from Instagram server-side and compare against the pre-task snapshot captured when the order was issued. Do not trust x5/x7. BIZ-07, STOR-12
  4. Replace x4 with a single-use server nonce bound to order_id, the device attestation and an expiry. BIZ-06, CRYP-03
  5. Widen set_order_stamp. Sign the canonical full body — account id, price, currency, server nonce, timestamp — not three fields. BIZ-04
  6. Enforce price and balance server-side on order/submitOrder.php; treat every client figure as display-only. BIZ-03
  7. Add rate limits and idempotency keys issued by the server on every money endpoint, plus anomaly detection on claim velocity. STOR-10, BIZ-10, BIZ-11
  8. Validate captcha tokens server-side against the provider's siteverify API, bound to the session and the action. STOR-08
  9. Bind VIP to a payment record and re-validate on every privileged call. BIZ-09

P2 — fix transport trust (weeks)

  1. Hard-code the base URL and pins. Remove Pin/PinActive from SharedPreferences and remove url/pin from ServerCheckModel. TRAN-02, CRYP-04
  2. Pin real SPKI values for the TLS leaf and intermediate, with a backup pin and an expiration date — not the APK signing certificate digest. CRYP-04
  3. Add network_security_config.xml so pinning survives Java-layer hooking. TRAN-03
  4. If pin rotation is genuinely needed, sign the ServerCheck payload with a public key embedded at build time and verify it natively before applying anything. TRAN-02, BIZ-12
  5. Ship updates through Play's in-app update API and drop update_url/download_link. BIZ-12
  6. Separate the trust domains: distinct OkHttp clients and pin sets for Instagram and for your backend, and never let one see the other's credentials. TRAN-05
  7. Move TLS verification into native code if it must resist instrumentation. CRYP-05, TRAN-01, TRAN-04

P3 — integrity, keys and process (months)

  1. Move to Play App Signing; retire the self-signed serial-1 key that runs to 2048. INTE-12
  2. Make Play Integrity mandatory server-side. Reject sensitive requests without a fresh, valid token; never let SND/RID decide whether to attest. INTE-08
  3. Use a server-issued random nonce per attestation and consume each token once, with a lifetime of minutes rather than six hours. INTE-09, INTE-10, CRED-10
  4. Validate appRecognitionVerdict and deviceRecognitionVerdict on the server instead of comparing Play Store certificates on the client. INTE-02
  5. Stop treating detection as prevention. Keep the maps/root/Frida checks as telemetry signals feeding server-side risk scoring, not as gates. INTE-03, INTE-04, INTE-05, INTE-06, INTE-07
  6. Replace the keyless cipher everywhere, including x2 and xr. CRYP-01
  7. Remove the hard-coded integrity constants d83fb7ce… and TFjMTZRk5rMHdTVR. CRYP-02, CRYP-03
  8. Per-install Keystore aliases with an attestation challenge. CRED-09
  9. Minimise data collection. Drop ANDROID_ID exfiltration, gate Crashlytics behind consent, scrub credentials from logs and exception messages, mark the notification VISIBILITY_PRIVATE. INTE-11, STOR-06, STOR-11
  10. Parse JSON properly. Replace every substring/indexOf branch in ia.s and ia.v with schema-validated parsing. STOR-12
  11. Authorise the task service per account and keep using in-process messaging rather than broadcasts. STOR-09
  12. Fix get_image.php: allow-list hosts server-side, block private and link-local ranges, disable redirects, cap size and content type. STOR-05
  13. Add per-session, revocable consent for each automation category, with a user-visible audit log. STOR-15

Existential — not fixable in code

  • Impersonating Instagram's private mobile API (b.i.instagram.com/api/v1/, i.instagram.com/api/v2/, the Bloks/CAA login flow) violates Instagram's Platform Policy and Terms of Use. The hard-coded x-ig-app-id, x-bloks-version-id and device fingerprint mean every user of the app is trivially identifiable and bannable. CRED-06, STOR-13
  • Forging x-ig-nav-chain, x-ig-salt-ids and x-fb-rmd, and running a warm-up burst with jittered timestamps, is engineered evasion of Instagram's fraud detection. There is no legitimate framing for it. CRED-07, CRED-08
  • Driving N user accounts in parallel from one device to generate paid engagement is the product. DoTasksService plus the SingleTasking flag is a farming engine by construction. BIZ-08
  • Automating DM interop and Threads extends the abuse past follower exchange into cross-product manipulation. STOR-14

20. Methodology & artefacts

Environment

No network access to Maven Central, Google Maven, JitPack, Debian mirrors or GitHub release assets was available, and no JDK could be installed — so apktool, aapt2, apksigner, jadx, dex2jar and radare2 were all out of reach. The entire analysis was built from first principles on a Python toolchain: androguard 4.1.4, lief 1.0.0, capstone 5.0.7, asn1crypto, plus frida 17.18.0 / frida-tools 14.10.4 for the dynamic lab.

Pipeline

  1. Manifest. Parsed the binary AXML directly with androguard; enumerated exported components, permissions, providers, meta-data and every application flag.
  2. DEX. The map_list is deliberately scrambled, so annotation-based recovery failed. Wrote a whole-DEX pseudocode dumper instead and produced readable Java for all 4,146 classes. Cross-checked obfuscated method bodies that the header-only decompiler dropped against full smali output (173 classes).
  3. Strings. Extracted all 21,862 DEX strings; grepped for SharedPreferences keys, addProperty JSON keys, .php paths, base64 literals, UUIDs and IG endpoint fragments.
  4. ARSC. Resolved @7F160000/@7F160001 from resources.arsc to res/Qq.xml and res/4j.xml and decoded both AXML files — this is what exposed the empty backup rules.
  5. Signing block. Wrote a manual APK Signature Scheme v2 parser: located the magic, read the pair length, walked the signer → certificates → signatures → public-key structure, and parsed the DER with asn1crypto. Confirmed the cert digest equals the embedded pin/tamper constant.
  6. ELF. lief for headers, segments, imports, exports and checksec across all six .so files. Confirmed JNI_OnLoad as the only export and the absence of crypto/TLS imports.
  7. JNI resolution. On x86 the JNINativeMethod table exists as raw VA pointers; a slot-pattern scan at file offset 0xd8fec resolved all 22 entries. The 64-bit builds build the table at runtime, so script 07 intercepts RegisterNatives instead.
  8. Defeating OLLVM. Static CFG tracing through the state dispatchers was infeasible, so the data path was attacked instead: detect every call $+5; pop reg; add reg,K thunk (943 found), compute a per-function base, and resolve .rodata references (760 resolved). The first attempt used a single global GOT base and produced mis-aligned strings; the per-thunk approach is what worked.
  9. String decryption. Exhaustive single-byte XOR sweep over keys 1–127 across every printable run, scoring candidate plaintexts. Two keys — 0x55 and 0x5A — recovered the complete secret set. Nested base64 literals were unwound separately.
  10. Cipher replication. Re-implemented glide.d.p()/q() in Python and verified round-trip on passwords, bearer tokens, TOTP seeds and URLs.
  11. Flow reconstruction. Traced the login chain (ia.v → ia.s → ia.i0), the WebView path (oa.l1), the task pipeline (DoTasksService → ja.e → ia.g), the claim path (ha.c), the dispatcher (androidx.fragment.app.e, 18 cases) and the integrity gate (d3.d.l → d8.f.a).
  12. PoC authoring. Wrote 10 Frida PoC scripts plus a shared helper (00_common.js), each annotated with the exact classes, offsets and literals it targets; verified all eleven parse cleanly under node --check.

Dead ends worth recording

  • The hts/ URL string set (e.g. hts/frbslgigp… → https://b.i.instagram.com/) is not a length-preserving permutation, not a greedy subsequence, not a half-interleave and not a per-index Caesar shift. It was cracked by the triple-base64 path in .rodata instead.
  • Retrofit annotations are unrecoverable here: method.get_annotations() returns nothing and direct annotation-parsing goes out of bounds because the DEX map_list is scrambled. Endpoint discovery had to come from the base64 literals at the call sites.
  • A single global GOT base for native string resolution produces mis-aligned and truncated strings. It must be per-function, derived from each PIC thunk.
  • The 64-bit .so files do not contain JNINativeMethod tables as raw VA pointers; only the x86 build does. Pointer scanning is pointless on arm64/x86_64.

21. Scope, ethics & responsible handling

What this document is

A defensive security research report on a single APK file supplied for analysis. It documents vulnerabilities, explains their mechanisms, and provides instrumentation intended to let a defender reproduce and then fix them.

What it is not

  • Not an attack service. No requests were sent to top.nivafollower.app, to any Instagram endpoint, or to any third party during this analysis. Everything here comes from static examination of the supplied binary plus instrumentation scripts written for the analyst's own device.
  • Not a guide to abusing Instagram. The private-API details are documented because they are the vulnerability surface — they show what the app does with credentials it should never have collected. They are not a recipe, and using them to automate accounts you do not own violates Instagram's Terms of Use and, in many jurisdictions, computer-misuse law.
  • Not a credential dump. No real user data is included. The credential-harvest script writes only to the analyst's own device, on accounts the analyst controls.

If you are the vendor

The P0 items in section 18 are achievable in days and close the mass-exposure paths. Two of them — removing the Token header and fixing the backup rules — are single-line changes. Treat the stored password and TOTP-seed columns as already compromised for every existing user and force a credential reset on next login.

If you are a user of this app

Change your Instagram password now, and revoke the session. This app has stored your password in a reversible form, may have forwarded it to a third-party server, may have uploaded it to your Google cloud backup, and has been acting on your account autonomously. Also: revoke third-party sessions in Instagram's Settings → Accounts Center → Password and security → Where you're logged in, re-enrol 2FA (the app may hold your current seed), and expect that the account may be actioned by Instagram for automation.

Disclosure

Findings are documented here for the party that commissioned the analysis. No vendor notification channel was exercised as part of this engagement, and no exploit was deployed against any live system. Coordinate disclosure with the vendor and with Meta's security team before publishing.
